Regulations 1, 17 and 19 for B.A./B.Sc. (T.D.C.) 
examination at pages 65, 76 and 77, respectively, of the 
Calendar, Volume II, 1967, shall read as under : — 


1. With effect from J 961, the course of instruction for 
B.A. and B.Sc, examinations shall extend over a period 
of three years and the examination shall be held in three 
parts, viz Pant I at the end of the course of first year. 
Part TI at the end of the course of second year and Part 
111 at the end of the course of third year. 

The examination in Part T and Part IT shall be held : — 

(a) annually in the months of April; 

(b) jor compartment candidates only , in the month 
of September ; 

or on such other date as may be fixed by the Syndicate, 
and examination in Part III shall be held twice a year 
in the months of April and September or on such other 
dates as may be fixed by the Syndicate. 

Provided <that students who pass in 1961 or who have 
already passed the Intermediate examination (Arts or 
Science) and desire to continue their studies, will join 
the Third Year class in 1961 for B.A. or B.Sc. examina- 
tion under the old Regulations.

at page 77 of the Calendar, Volume II, 1967 shall read as 
under ; — 

20. A candidate who obtains **35 per cent of the 
aggregate number of marks in the three main subjects, 
but has failed in one subject only, obtaining not less 
than 20*** per cent of the marks in ithat subject, shall h- 
permitted to appear in that subject only at the next two 
examinations and if he passes at either Of those examina- 
tions, he shall be deemed to have passed the examination. 

A candidate to whom this concession is granted shall 
be eligible to join the next higher class, but if he fails 
to qualify in the compartment subject at the supplemen- 
tary examination, he shall be permitted to appear again 
in that subject along with the examination for the next 
Part at the next annual examination and if he fails to 
qualify in ihe compartment subject even at the second 
attempt, his result 'ror Part II or Part 1J1, as the case 
may he, shall be cancelled.

and Practice of Medicine, excepting subjects which are 
exclusively Surgical and shall be submitted within a

thesis was approved, provided that the period may be 
extended in exceptional circumstances.

Regulations 12 and 13 relating to use of unfair means 
in tbc University Examination at pages 106-108 of the 
Calendar, Volume I, 1967, shall read as under:- — 

12(a) If during a University examination a candidate 
is found tuning in his possession or accessible to him 
papers, books, or notes, which do not relate to the sub- 
ject of examination and which could not possibly be of 
any assistance to him, no action may be taken against 
him. But the case must be reported to the Registrar 
with necessary papers. 

The Registrar need not report such a case to the 
Standing Committee. 

12(b) If at a University examination* but before the 
question paper is dls f ributed, a Candidate voluntarily 
surrenders to the Superintendent or any other member 
of the supervisory staff papers , books or notes in his 
possession and not found or detected by a member of 
the supervisory staff , no action may be taken against 
him , provided he has not made any use of them , But 
the case shall be reported to the Registrar . 

12(c) If during a University examination a candidate 
Is found having in his possession or accessible to him 
papers, books or no f es due to inadvertence hut which 
papers, hooks or notes could be of assistance to him he 
may be debarred from passing in that paper as a disciplU 
nary measure without any implication of moral turpi- 
tude. 

12(d) If during a University examination a candidate 
is found having in his possession papers, books or notes 


or js found having written notes on any part of the 
clothes worn by him or on any pari of his body or table 
or desk or is found in possession of foot-rule and/or 
instruments like scCsquarcs, protractors, slide rules, etc. 
with notes written on theni and which notes, papers or 
books or the material written on toot rules or instru- 
ments, or clothes worn by him or on any part of his body 
or table or desk,, etc. are helpful to him during the 
examination* and lotdd he of assistance to him and if his 
possession of such material is found to be malaftde he 
shall be disqualified from appearing in any University 
examination for two years, including that in which he is 
found guilty if he is a candidate for an examination hp!d 
once a year, or for four examinations, including that in 
which he is found guilty, if he js a candidate for an 
examination held twice a year. 

Ufa) If during a University examination , a candi- 
date is found talkiug to another candidate or any person 
inside or outside the examination hall, during the exami- 
nation hours , without the permission of a member of the 
supervisory staff, before he has handed over his answer 
book, his answer-bopk for that particular paper shall be 
cancelled. 

13(b) If an answer-book of an examinee show’s pr it 
is otherwise established that he has received or attempted 
to receive help from or given help or attempted to give 
help to another candidate he shall be disqualified from 
appearing in any University Examination for two years, 
including that in which he is found guilty if he is a candi- 
date for an examination held once a year, or for foui 
examinations, including that in which he is found guilty, 
if he is a candidate for an examination held twice a 
year. 

13(c) If during a University Examination a candi- 
date is found having copied or indulging in copying 
from any paper , book or notes or if he has allowed or 
is found allowing any other candidate to copy any matter 
from his answer hook or to have in any manner rendered 
any assistance to another candidate in solving a question 
or a part of question set in the question paper or if he 
is found to have been rendered assistance in solving a 
question or a part of question set in the question paper, 
he shall be disqualified for a period of iwo years, in- 
cluding that in which he is found guilty if he is a candi- 
date for an examination held once a year, or for four 
examinations, including that in which he is found guilt w 
if he is a candidate for an examination held twice a 
year. 

13(d) If a candidate during an examination of the 
University is found swallowing or attempting to swallow 
a note or paper or runs away with it or is guilty of caus- 
ing disappearance or destroying any such material , he 
shall be disqualified from appearing in any University 
Examination for two years , including that in which he is 
found guilty if he is a candidate for an examine don held 
once a year, or for four examinations, including that in 
which he is found guilty, if he is a candidate for an 
examination held twice a year . 

13(e) If during a University Examination a candi- 
date is found consulting books , note-books , or papers , 
or any other matter found with him while outside the 
examination hall but during the examination hours and 
before he has handed over his answer-book to the Supci- 
intendent or any offier member of the supervisors staff, 
he shall be disqualified from appearing in any Universitv 
ExaminaUon for two years , including that in which he 
is found guilty if he is a candidate for an examination 
held once a year , or for four examinations. Including 
that in which he is found guilty , if he is a candidate for
